induced by the ring homomorphism
\theta_{\textup{Bun}}^{*}
. Using the additivity
\mathbb{V}_{G^{\prime}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{G_{\textup{ad}}}\oplus\mathbb{V}_{A}
, we thus reduce to consider separately the case
G
is adjoint (or more generally semisimple) and
G
is a torus.

4.2.5.
The torus case

Let
G=A
be a torus. In this case,
\mathbb{V}_{A}=\mathrm{R}^{2}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}A}[-2]
, which is canonically a summand of
\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}A}
by (
4.1.3
). We define
\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{\textup{aug},A}
to be the composition

\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathbb{V}_{A})_{+}\subset\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}A})_{+}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{A}}\mathrm{H}^{>0}(\textup{Bun}_{A}^{\omega})\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Gr}^{1}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{A}^{\omega}).

4.2.6.
The semisimple case

Let
G
be semisimple. Let
\mathrm{R}^{>0}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G}=F^{1}_{\textup{aug}}(\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})
be the direct sum of the positive degree summands under the canonical splitting (
4.1.3
). By construction we have a canonical map that is a surjection in each degree,

\mathrm{R}^{>0}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G}\to\mathbb{V}_{G}.

The desired map (
4.2.9
) is characterized by the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2.7
.

There is a unique map
\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{\textup{aug},G}
making the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathrm{R}^{>0}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{>0}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathbb{V}_{G})_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{\textup{aug},G}}
\textstyle{\textup{Gr}^{1}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})}

Here
\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathrm{R}^{>0}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})_{+}\subset\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathrm{R}^{>0}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})
is the positive degree part.

Proof.

Uniqueness is clear because the vertical maps are surjective. It remains to check that

(4.2.10)

\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{G}
sends
\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})_{+}
to
F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})
.

For this we compare
G
with the constant group situation.

Let
\nu:X^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{\textup{Out}}\to X
as in §
4.1.7
, so that
\nu^{*}\mathbb{B}G_{\textup{ad}}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}}\times X^{\prime}
. This induces a canonical map
\varphi:\textup{Bun}_{G}\to\textup{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}},X^{\prime}}
by pulling back
G
-bundles along
\nu
and pushout along
\mathbb{B}G\to\mathbb{B}G_{\textup{ad}}\cong\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}}
. We have a commutative diagram

(4.2.11)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\times\textup{Bun}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textup{ev}}}
\scriptstyle{\textup{id}_{X^{\prime}}\times\varphi}
\textstyle{\nu^{*}\mathbb{B}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\times\textup{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}},X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{ev}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}}}

Here
\widetilde{\textup{ev}}
is the base change of
\textup{ev}:X\times\textup{Bun}_{G}\to\mathbb{B}G
along
\nu
. Applying the discussion of the constant group case in §
4.2.3
, (
4.2.11
) induces a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}_{*}(X^{\prime})\otimes R^{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}},X^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{>0}(\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\textup{ad}},X^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{ev}^{\omega}_{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{>0}(\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G})}

Here the map
\alpha
is the canonical quotient to
\textup{Out}(\mathbb{G})
-coinvariants. Since
\varphi^{*}
respects the augmentation filtrations, and
\alpha
maps
\mathrm{H}_{*}(X^{\prime})\otimes F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}R^{W}
surjectively to
\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\ell,\mathbb{B}G})
, it suffices to check that (
4.2.10
) holds for the constant group
\mathbb{G}\times X^{\prime}
over
X^{\prime}
.

We henceforth rename
X^{\prime}
to
X
and let
G=\mathbb{G}\times X
, where
\mathbb{G}
is semisimple. In this case, (
4.2.10
) is equivalent to: for any
f_{1},f_{2}\in R^{W}_{+}
and any
z\in\textup{H}_{*}({X})
, we have
(f_{1}f_{2})^{z}\in F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})
. Indeed, let
\Delta_{*}(z)\in\textup{H}_{*}({X\times X})\cong\textup{H}_{*}({X})\otimes\textup{H}_{*}({X})
be the image of
z
under the diagonal map for
X
. Write
\Delta_{*}(z)=\sum_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}\otimes z^{\prime\prime}_{i}
for
z^{\prime}_{i},z^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in\textup{H}_{*}({X})
. Then

(4.2.12)

(f_{1}f_{2})^{z}=\sum_{i}f_{1}^{z^{\prime}_{i}}f_{2}^{z^{\prime\prime}_{i}}.

Since
G
is semisimple,
f_{1},f_{2}
have cohomological degrees
\geq 4
while
z^{\prime}_{i},z^{\prime\prime}_{i}
have homological degrees
\leq 2
, so
f_{1}^{z^{\prime}_{i}}
and
f_{2}^{z^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
both have positive degrees, hence belong to
\textup{H}^{>0}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. Therefore
(f_{1}f_{2})^{z}\in F^{2}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. This proves (
4.2.10
) and finishes the proof.
∎

Now we can state the Atiyah-Bott formula for the cohomology of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
.

Theorem 4.2.8
.

For any
\omega\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
, the
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra map

(4.2.13)

\textup{Sym}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}}^{\blacktriangleright}(\mathrm{H}_{*}(X;\mathbb{V}_{G})_{+})\rightarrow\textup{Gr}^{\blacktriangleright}_{\textup{aug}}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})

induced by (
4.2.9
) is an isomorphism.

4.2.9.
The constant group case

When
G=G_{0}\times X
, choose a basis of
\textup{H}_{*}({X})
:

z_{0}=1\in\textup{H}_{0}({X}),\quad z_{1},\cdots,z_{2g}\in\textup{H}_{1}({X})\mbox{ and }z_{2g+1}=[X]\in\textup{H}_{2}({X}).

Choose also homogeneous free generators
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}
of
R^{W}
as a polynomial ring. Then Theorem
4.2.8
implies that
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
is the polynomial ring over
\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}}
with free generators

(4.2.14)

f_{i}^{z_{j}}|_{\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}},\quad 0\leq j\leq 2g+1,1\leq i\leq n,\mbox{ such that $(\deg f_{i},|z_{j}|)\neq(2,2)$.}

(Here
|z_{j}|
is the homological degree of
z_{j}
.) In particular, we get canonical isomorphisms between the cohomology rings of different components of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
, under which the classes with the same name
f^{z}
(in the constant group case) correspond to each other.

Remark 4.2.10
.

When
X
and
G
are defined over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
,
\textup{Bun}_{G}
is also defined over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
. The Frobenius
Fr
acts on
\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G,\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}})
and induces a map
\textup{Bun}_{G,\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}^{\omega}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G,\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}^{\textup{Fr}(\omega)}
. From the construction of (
4.2.13
), we obtain a commutative diagram

(4.2.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Sym}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}}^{\blacktriangleright}(\mathrm{H}_{*}(X_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}};\mathbb{V}_{G})_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Gr}_{\textup{aug}}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G,\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}^{\textup{Fr}(\omega)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Sym}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}}^{\blacktriangleright}(\mathrm{H}_{*}(X_{\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}};\mathbb{V}_{G})_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Gr}_{\textup{aug}}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G,\overline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}}^{\omega})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Fr}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Fr}^{*}}

In particular, if
\textup{Fr}(\omega)=\omega
then the isomorphism (
4.2.13
) is automatically
Fr
-equivariant.

4.2.11.
Prior results

There have been many partial results towards Theorem
4.2.8
in the literature. The formula is inspired by a formula for closely related moduli spaces of
G
-bundles on a Riemann surface, for split semisimple
G
, due to Atiyah–Bott
[
AB83
]
. The analogous formula for
\textup{Bun}_{G}
on a complex algebraic curve, again for split semisimple
G
, was established by Teleman in
[
Tel98
]
.

Turning towards
X/\mathbf{F}_{q}
, in the case where
G\rightarrow X
is a constant semisimple group scheme (i.e., pulled back from a semisimple group
G_{0}/\mathbf{F}_{q}
), Theorem
4.2.8
is proved in
[
HS10
]
. In the case where
G\rightarrow X
has simply connected semisimple generic fiber (which implies that
\textup{Bun}_{G}
is geometrically connected), it is proved in
[
Gai19
]
based on the work of Gaitsgory–Lurie
[
GL14
]
; the same result appears in
[
Ho21
, Theorem 6.1.3]
. We will bootstrap from this last result to prove Theorem
4.2.8
in the general reductive case.

4.3.
Propagation of the Atiyah–Bott formula

We prove that Theorem
4.2.8
is stable under certain operations. We denote by
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
the neutral component (i.e., the connected component of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
containing the trivial
G
-bundle).

4.3.1.
Products

Let
G_{1},G_{2}
be reductive group schemes over
X
, and
G=G_{1}\times_{X}G_{2}
. Then Theorem
4.2.8
holds for both
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, if and only if it holds for
G
. Indeed, we have
\mathbb{B}G\cong(\mathbb{B}G_{1})\times_{X}(\mathbb{B}G_{2})
and
\textup{Bun}_{G}\cong\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}\times\textup{Bun}_{G_{2}}
. Thus both sides of (
4.2.13
) for
G
can be written canonically as the tensor product of their counterparts for
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
.

4.3.2.
Central isogenies

Let
\theta\colon G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
be a central isogeny, with kernel
\Gamma
. It induces a map
v:\textup{Bun}_{G}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}
.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

The map
v^{0}\colon\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
is surjective.

We record some preliminary Lemmas which will be used in the proof.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

Let
f\colon G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime}
be any homomorphism of groups over
S
whose kernel
F:=\ker(f)
is central in
G_{1}
. (In particular,
F
is abelian, so that the category
\mathbb{B}F(S)
of
F
-torsors on
S
has a natural abelian group structure in groupoids). Then the fiber of the category
\mathbb{B}G_{1}(S)
over a given
G^{\prime}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{B}G^{\prime}(S)
, is itself a torsor for
\mathbb{B}F(S)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}
be a
G_{1}
-torsor equipped with an isomorphism
\tau_{0}\colon\mathcal{F}_{0}\times^{G_{1}}G^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
. If
\mathcal{F}
is another
G_{1}
-torsor equipped with an isomorphism
\tau\colon\mathcal{F}\times^{G_{1}}G^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, then we claim that the sheaf
\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}((\mathcal{F}_{0},\tau_{0}),(\mathcal{F},\tau))
of isomorphisms from
\mathcal{F}_{0}
to
\mathcal{F}
intertwining the identifications
\tau_{0}
and
\tau
, is an
F
-torsor. Clearly
F
acts on it; to check that it is an
F
-torsor, we can work locally, thus reducing to the case where
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0}
are trivial, where the assertion is clear.
∎

Lemma 4.3.5
.

Let
f\colon G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
be a central isogeny of reductive groups over a curve. Then there is a factorization
G\rightarrow G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime}
such that
\ker(G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime})
is an induced torus.

Proof.

Let
K:=\ker(f)
, a finite flat group scheme over
X_{\overline{k}}
of multiplicative type. Then the Cartier dual
K^{D}=\underline{\textup{Hom}}(K,\mathbb{G}_{m})
is a finite étale group scheme over
X_{\overline{k}}
. By choosing a surjection onto
K^{D}
from a permutation module for
\pi_{1}(X_{\overline{k}})
and then forming Cartier duals, we can embed
K
in an induced torus
T
. Now we let
G_{1}:=(G\times_{X}T)/K
, where
K
acts diagonally as

k\cdot(g,t)=(gk,k^{-1}t).

We then have a map
G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime}
given by
(g,t)\mapsto f(g)
. Clearly the kernel is
(K\times_{X}T)/K\cong T
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.3.3
.

In the proof, we base change the situation to
\overline{k}
without changing notation.

Choose a factorization
G\rightarrow G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime}
as in Lemma
4.3.5
. Let
T:=\ker(G_{1}\rightarrow G^{\prime})
, which by assumption is an induced torus. First we claim that the map
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}
is surjective. It suffices to show this on geometric points, so let
\kappa
be a separably closed field over
k
. Then the map
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}(\kappa)\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}(\kappa)
is identified with

H^{1}(X_{\kappa};G_{1})\rightarrow H^{1}(X_{\kappa};G^{\prime}).

The obstruction classes for this map lie in
H^{2}(X_{\kappa};T)
, which vanishes because
T
is an induced torus (by Shapiro’s Lemma and the vanishing of Brauer groups of curves over separably closed fields
[
Gro68
, Corollary 5.8]
).
This shows that
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}
. Lemma
4.3.4
shows that this map is a
\textup{Bun}_{T}
-torsor, so we obtain an isomorphism
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}/\textup{Bun}_{T}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}
. Also, we observe that the map
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
is surjective. Indeed, the exact sequence
\pi_{1}^{\textup{alg}}(T/X)\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\textup{alg}}(G_{1}/X)\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\textup{alg}}(G^{\prime}/X)\rightarrow 0
plus (
4.2.2
) show that for a geometric point of
\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
, any lift to
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}
can be translated to
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}
by the action of
\textup{Bun}_{T}
.

Let
D:=G_{1}/G
, so that
G\rightarrow G_{1}\rightarrow D
is an exact sequence. Then we have a Cartesian square

(4.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}}
pt
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{D}}

where the bottom horizontal arrow is the map induced by trivial
D
-bundle. Let
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\natural}\subset\textup{Bun}_{G}
be the preimage of
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G_{1}}
under
\textup{Bun}_{G}\to\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}
. Then
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\natural}
is a union of connected components of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
. We have a Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}}
pt
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{D}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

The map
d
is equivariant under the translation actions of
\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}
. Since
T\to D
is an isogeny, the induced map
\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}\to\textup{Bun}_{D}^{0}
is surjective, hence the translation action of
\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}
on
\textup{Bun}_{D}^{0}
is transitively. This shows that
d
is surjective, and that
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\natural}\to\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G_{1}}/\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}
is surjective. We observed earlier that
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
is surjective; since this map factors through
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}/\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}
, we deduce that
\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}/\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
is also surjective. Composing this with the surjection
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\natural}\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G_{1}}^{0}/\textup{Bun}_{T}^{0}
, we conclude that

(4.3.2)

The map
v^{\natural}:\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{G}\to\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G^{\prime}}
is surjective.

In particular, we learn that the map
v^{\prime}\colon v^{-1}(\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G^{\prime}})\rightarrow\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G^{\prime}}
is surjective. By Lemma
4.3.4
,
v^{\prime}
is a torsor for the abelian group stack
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
. In particular, it is flat. At the same time, we see that
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
is finite by presenting
\Gamma
as the kernel of an isogeny of tori. Therefore,
v^{\prime}
is also finite. Restricting
v^{\prime}
to the connected component
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
, we see that the map
v^{0}\colon\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G^{\prime}}
is also finite and flat. This implies that the image of
v^{0}
is both open and closed, and clearly non-empty, hence must be everything.

∎

Return to the central isogeny
\theta\colon G\rightarrow G^{\prime}
with kernel
\Gamma
. Note that
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
is an abelian group stack. By the constructions of §
4.1.12
,
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
acts on
\textup{Bun}_{G}
such that the map
\textup{Bun}_{G}\to\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}
induced by
\theta
is
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
-invariant. Let
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}^{\natural}
be the subgroup stack of
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}
that preserves the neutral component
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G}
, then we have a canonical map

(4.3.3)

\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}/\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}\to\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}.

Corollary 4.3.6
.

The map (
4.3.3
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Lemma
4.3.4
shows that
\textup{Bun}_{G}
is a
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}^{\natural}
-torsor over its image. Then it suffices to see that the map
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}\rightarrow\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
is surjective, which is Proposition
4.3.3
.
∎

We have a corresponding classifying map

f\colon\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}.

Each direct image
\mathrm{R}^{i}f_{*}(\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
is a local system on
\mathbb{B}\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}
with stalk
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G})
, together with the action of
\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma})
coming from the action of
\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}
on
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G}
.

Lemma 4.3.7
.

Suppose that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
G
. Then the action of
\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma})
on
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0})
is trivial.

Proof.

According to the Atiyah–Bott formula for
G
,
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}^{0}_{G})
is generated by the Künneth components of
G
-Chern classes of the universal
G
-bundle on
X\times\textup{Bun}_{G}
. But we saw in Corollary
4.1.15
that twisting by
\Gamma
-torsors has trivial effect on
G
-Chern classes.
∎

Example 4.3.8
.

Consider the universal cover
\textup{SL}_{2}=G\rightarrow G^{\prime}=\textup{PGL}_{2}
. Then
\Gamma=\mu_{2}
and
\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}=\textup{Bun}_{\Gamma}^{\natural}
can be identified with the moduli stack of 2-torsion line bundles. The action on
\textup{Bun}_{\textup{SL}_{2}}
is via tensoring with 2-torsion line bundles. Those all have trivial Chern class, so the action on the Atiyah–Bott description is evidently trivial.

Corollary 4.3.9
.

Suppose that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
.
Then
f
induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}),

and Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}
.

Proof.

From the presentation
\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}=[\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}/\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}]
we have a spectral sequence

\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathbb{B}\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma};\mathrm{H}^{j}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}))\implies\mathrm{H}^{i+j}(\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}).

Since
\Gamma
is finite and of multiplicative type,
\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}
is a finite abelian group stack, so the higher cohomology of
\mathbb{B}\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}
vanishes with rational coefficients. The spectral sequence therefore degenerates. Furthermore, since
\mathrm{H}^{j}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0})
is the trivial local system on
\textup{Bun}^{\natural}_{\Gamma}
by Lemma
4.3.7
, we obtain that the pullback map

\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0})

is an isomorphism. Moreover, it is clear from construction and Lemma
4.1.5
that this isomorphism is compatible with the formulation of Theorem
4.2.8
.
∎

4.3.10.
Pure inner twisting

Let
G\rightarrow S
be a reductive group scheme and
\mathcal{P}\in\mathbb{B}G(S)
. We may view
\mathcal{P}
as a
G
-torsor in the étale site of
S
. Let
G^{\prime}=\underline{\textup{Aut}}_{S}(\mathcal{P})
be the (internal) automorphism group of
\mathcal{P}
(as a
G
-torsor) in the étale site of
S
. Then by general nonsense,
G^{\prime}
is a twist of
G
in the étale site of
S
. Furthermore, the
pure inner twisting
construction
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{S}^{G}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{P})
carries
G
-torsors to
G^{\prime}
-torsors, and induces an equivalence of groupoids

\mathbb{B}G(S)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}G^{\prime}(S).

Now let
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
be a connected component of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
. Fix a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{P}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}(k)
. Letting
G^{\prime}=\underline{\textup{Aut}}_{X}(\mathcal{P})
be the corresponding pure inner twist of
G
, the pure inner twisting construction
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{S}^{G}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{P})
induces an isomorphism

\textup{Bun}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}

carrying
\mathcal{P}
to the trivial
G^{\prime}
-torsor, hence in particular carrying

\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}.

Lemma 4.3.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a class of connected reductive group schemes over
X
preserved by pure inner twisting, e.g.,
\mathcal{C}=\{\text{semisimple }G/X\}
or
\mathcal{C}=\{\text{connected reductive }G/X\}
. Suppose Theorem
4.2.8
holds for the neutral components
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
of all groups
G\in\mathcal{C}
. Then Theorem
4.2.8
holds for all
G\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Pure inner twisting by
\mathcal{P}
induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{B}G\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}G^{\prime}
of classifying stacks over
X
, such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\textup{Bun}_{G^{\prime}}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}G^{\prime}}
ev
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
ev

commutes. Then it is clear that Theorem
4.2.8
for the bottom row implies Theorem
4.2.8
for the top row.
∎

4.4.
The simply connected case

In the case where
G
is semisimple with simply connected generic fiber,
\textup{Bun}_{G}
is connected and Theorem
4.2.8
is already proved in
[
Gai19
, (0.7)]
, and later is reproved by similar methods in
[
Ho21
, Theorem 6.13]
.

4.5.
The semisimple case

We prove that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for semisimple groups.

Let
G
be a semisimple group scheme over
X
. Let
G_{\textup{sc}}\rightarrow G
be the simply connected cover. Then we saw in §
4.4
that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}_{G_{\textup{sc}}}
. From Corollary
4.3.9
we obtain that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
, for all semisimple groups
G
. Then from Lemma
4.3.11
, we obtain that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for all semisimple groups.

4.6.
The torus case

We will prove Theorem
4.2.8
when
G=T
is a torus over
X
. Applying Lemma
4.3.11
to
\mathcal{C}=\{T\}
, it suffices to prove Theorem
4.2.8
for the neutral component
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{T}
.

4.6.1.
Split tori

First suppose
T\cong\mathbb{T}\times X
is split. Then the result is classical, but let us spell it out. By the additive nature of both sides of (
4.2.13
), it suffices to treat the case
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{G}_{m}
, in which case
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{T}=\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X}
. Upon choosing
x_{0}\in X(k)
, we have

\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X}\cong\textup{Jac}_{X}\times_{k}\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}.

Here
\textup{Jac}_{X}
is the moduli of degree zero line bundles on
X
with a trivialization at
x_{0}
. It follows that

(4.6.1)

\textup{Gr}^{1}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X})\cong\textup{H}^{1}(\textup{Jac}_{X})[-1]\oplus\textup{H}^{2}(\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})[-2],

and the natural map

\textup{Sym}^{\bullet}\textup{Gr}^{1}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X})\to\textup{Gr}^{\bullet}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X})

is an isomorphism. Thus, to prove Theorem
4.2.8
in this case, it suffices to show that

\mathfrak{ev}^{0}_{\textup{aug},\mathbb{G}_{m}}:(\textup{H}_{*}(X)\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})_{+}\to\textup{Gr}^{1}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}(\textup{Pic}^{0}_{X})

is an isomorphism.

We have
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}=\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}[-2](-1)
, so that

(4.6.2)

(\textup{H}_{*}(X)\otimes\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})_{+}=\mathrm{H}_{1}(X)[-1](-1)\oplus\mathrm{H}_{0}(X)[-2](-1).

Under the isomorphisms (
4.6.1
) and (
4.6.2
), the map
\mathfrak{ev}^{0}_{\textup{aug},\mathbb{G}_{m}}
respects each direct summand. On the first summand, as a map
\mathrm{H}_{1}(X)(-1)\to\textup{H}^{1}(\textup{Jac}_{X})
, or equivalently, an element in
\textup{H}^{1}(X)\otimes\textup{H}^{1}(\textup{Jac}_{X})(1)
, it is the Künneth component of
c_{1}(\mathcal{L}^{\textup{univ}})
for the universal line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\textup{univ}}
on
X\times\textup{Jac}_{X}
. This shows that
\mathfrak{ev}^{0}_{\textup{aug},\mathbb{G}_{m}}
is an isomorphism on the first summand. On the second summand, if we compose with restriction to
x_{0}
, the evaluation map becomes the identity map
\textup{H}^{2}(\mathbb{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})[-2](-1)\to\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}[-2](-1)=\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
. This shows
\mathfrak{ev}^{0}_{\textup{aug},\mathbb{G}_{m}}
is an isomorphism, and completes the proof for split tori.

4.6.2.
Induced tori

Let
\nu:Y\to X
be a finite étale map,
T_{0}
a torus over
Y
, and
T=\textup{Res}_{Y/X}T_{0}
be the Weil restriction, a torus over
X
. We claim that if Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
T_{0}
and
Y
, then it holds for
T
and
X
. Indeed,
\mathrm{H}_{*}(Y,\mathbb{V}_{T_{0}})
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}_{*}(X,\mathbb{V}_{T})
, and
\textup{Bun}_{T,X}
is canonically isomorphic to
\textup{Bun}_{T_{0},Y}
.

In particular, by the split tori case already proved, Theorem
4.2.8
holds for any induced torus
T=\textup{Res}_{Y/X}(\mathbb{T}\times Y)
, where
Y
is finite étale over
X
.

4.6.3.
General tori

In general,
T
splits over some finite étale Galois
\nu\colon Y\rightarrow X
, so that
\nu^{*}T\cong\mathbb{T}\times Y
for some torus
\mathbb{T}
over
\textup{Spec}\ k
. Then we have a canonical map
\alpha:T\to T^{\prime}=\textup{Res}_{Y/X}(\mathbb{T}\times Y)
and a norm map
\beta:T^{\prime}\to T
such that the composition
\beta\circ\alpha=[N]
, where
N=\deg(\nu)
. Therefore one can find a torus
S
over
X
together with an isogeny
T^{\prime}\to T\times_{X}S
. Since Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{T^{\prime}}
by §
4.6.2
, it also holds for
\textup{Bun}_{T\times_{X}S}^{0}
by Corollary
4.3.9
, hence also for
\textup{Bun}^{0}_{T}
by §
4.3.1
. This finishes the proof in the torus case.

4.7.
The reductive case

If
G
is reductive, let
Z^{\circ}
be the connected center of
G
and
G_{\textup{der}}
the derived subgroup of
G
. Then the map
Z^{\circ}\times_{X}G_{\textup{der}}\rightarrow G
is a central isogeny. Theorem
4.2.8
has been established for
Z^{\circ}
and
G_{\textup{der}}
, hence also for
Z^{\circ}\times_{X}G_{\textup{der}}
. By Corollary
4.3.9
, Theorem
4.2.8
holds for
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
.

At this point we know that Theorem
4.2.8
holds for the geometric neutral component
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{0}
for every reductive
G
. Then by its propagation under pure inner twisting construction (Lemma
4.3.11
) it holds for all the geometric connected components of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
for every reductive
G
.

5.
Arithmetic volume of shtukas for split groups

Fix a prime
p
. Henceforth our base field will be
k:=\mathbf{F}_{q}
where
q
is a power of
p
. Let
X
be a smooth, projective, geometrically connected curve over
k
, of genus
g
.

In this section, we formulate and prove our main formula for the “arithmetic volume” of the moduli stack of shtukas for a
split
reductive group scheme over
X
. (The nonsplit case will be treated later, in §
7
.) As we only work with constant group schemes over
X
in this section, we will denote by
G
a connected reductive group over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
(which was denoted by
\mathbb{G}
in §
4
); the discussions in §
4
are applied to the constant group scheme
G\times X
over
X
.

5.1.
Hecke correspondences

Below we write
\mathbb{V}_{G}
as
\mathbb{V}
.
Let
\omega\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
. Theorem
4.2.8
gives a bigraded isomorphism of algebras

(5.1.1)

\textup{AB}_{\omega}:\textup{Sym}^{\blacktriangleright}((\textup{H}_{*}({X})\otimes\mathbb{V})_{+})\to\textup{Gr}^{\blacktriangleright}_{\textup{aug}}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}).

We recall the notation
f^{z}\in\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
from §
4.2.3
for
f\in R^{W}
and
z\in\textup{H}_{*}({X})
.

We will apply the general result in §
3
to the Hecke correspondence for
\textup{Bun}_{G}
. Let
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)^{+}
be a dominant coweight. Consider the Hecke correspondence

\textstyle{\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{0}}
\scriptstyle{h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{X}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}}

that classifies modifications
(x,\mathcal{F}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1})
at one moving point
x\in X
of type
equal
to
\mu
(rather than
\leq\mu
).
7
7

7

Our main theorems will treat the case where
\mu
is minuscule, in which case there is no distinction between these notions.

For a connected component
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
, let
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\subset\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
be the preimage of
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
under
h_{0}
. Then
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
has a natural correspondence structure

(5.1.2)

\textstyle{{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{X}}
\scriptstyle{h_{0}}
\scriptstyle{h_{1}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}}

where
\omega^{\prime}=\omega+\overline{\mu}
, and
\overline{\mu}
denotes the image of
\mu
in
\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}})
, the quotient of
\mathbb{X}_{*}(\mathbb{T})
by the coroot lattice, cf (
4.2.1
).

5.1.1.
Tautological classes

Consider
X\times\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
with projection to the two factors denoted
q_{X}:X\times\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to X
and
p_{\mathrm{Hk}}:X\times\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
. Let
\Gamma(p_{X})\subset X\times\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
be the graph of
p_{X}:\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to X
. Let
\mathcal{F}^{\textup{univ}}
be the universal
G
-bundle over
X\times\textup{Bun}_{G}
. We have two
G
-bundles on
X\times\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}

\mathcal{F}_{0}:=(q_{X}\times h_{0})^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\textup{univ}},\quad\mathcal{F}_{1}:=(q_{X}\times h_{1})^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\textup{univ}}

together with the universal Hecke modification of type
\mu
along
\Gamma(p_{X})

(5.1.3)

\varphi^{\textup{univ}}:\mathcal{F}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}.

By Proposition
3.1.4
, the
G
-torsor
\mathcal{F}_{0}|_{\Gamma(p_{X})}
carries a canonical
P_{\mu}
-reduction classified by a map

(5.1.4)

\textup{ev}_{\mu}:\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}.

We emphasize that the
P_{\mu}
-reduction comes from
\mathcal{F}_{0}|_{\Gamma(p_{X})}
, not
\mathcal{F}_{1}|_{\Gamma(p_{X})}
.

Restricting to the
\omega
-component, which we denote by
\textup{ev}^{\omega}_{\mu}
, it induces a ring homomorphism

(5.1.5)

\textup{ev}^{\omega,*}_{\mu}:R^{W_{\mu}}\cong\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}})\to\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

which is easily seen to be injective. Combining this with pullback
p_{X}^{*}
along the leg map, we get a ring homomorphism

(5.1.6)

\textup{H}^{*}({X})\otimes R^{W_{\mu}}\to\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}).

The image of this map can be regarded as “tautological classes on
\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
”.

5.1.2.
Tautological endomorphisms of cohomology

Now assume that
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
is minuscule and dominant. Let
D_{\mu}:=\langle{2\rho,\mu}\rangle=\dim G/P_{\mu}
. We consider classes in
\textup{H}^{2D_{\mu}+2}({\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
which are the image under (
5.1.6
) of

\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime},\mbox{ where }\eta\in R^{W_{\mu}}_{2(D_{\mu}+1)},\eta^{\prime}\in R^{W_{\mu}}_{2D_{\mu}}.

Let
\omega^{\prime}=\omega+\overline{\mu}\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
, where we recall that
\overline{\mu}
is the image of
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
in
\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
. Consider the degree-preserving map

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu}^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}:\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})

\displaystyle\to\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle\mapsto h_{0*}((\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime})\cdot h_{1}^{*}\theta).

The pushforward map on cohomology
h_{0*}
is defined because
h_{0}
is smooth and proper (recall that
\mu
is assumed to be minuscule). The map
\Gamma_{\mu}^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}
is degree-preserving because the relative dimension of
h_{0}
is
D_{\mu}+1
.

5.1.3.
Variant on compactly supported cohomology

With the same notation, we define

(5.1.9)

\displaystyle{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}_{\mu}:\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})

\displaystyle\to\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})

(5.1.10)

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle\mapsto h_{1*}((\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime})\cdot h_{0}^{*}\theta).

The definition is arranged so that under the Poincaré duality pairing

(5.1.11)

\langle{-,-}\rangle:\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\times\textup{H}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}-i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\to\textup{H}_{c}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\cong\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}(-\dim\textup{Bun}_{G})

the map
{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}_{\mu}
is adjoint to
\Gamma^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}_{\mu}
, i.e.,

(5.1.12)

\langle{{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}_{\mu}(\alpha),\beta}\rangle=\langle{\alpha,\Gamma^{\eta+\xi\eta^{\prime}}_{\mu}(\beta)}\rangle

for all
\alpha\in\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
and
\beta\in\textup{H}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}-i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})
.

5.2.
Arithmetic volume of the moduli of shtukas

We can now define the moduli spaces of shtukas and the notion of arithmetic volume of their tautological classes.

5.2.1.
Moduli of shtukas

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{r})
be a sequence of minuscule dominant coweights of
G
that is
admissible
in the sense that

(5.2.1)

\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\cdots+\mu_{r}\text{ lies in the coroot lattice of $G$.}

In other words, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{r}\overline{\mu}_{i}=0\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
, cf. (
4.2.1
).

Let
\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
be the fiber product

\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{1}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{2}}\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}}\cdots\times_{\textup{Bun}_{G}}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{r}}

that classifies iterated modifications
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{r},\mathcal{F}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{r})
, where
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{i}
is a modification along
x_{i}
of type
\mu_{i}
. For
0\leq i\leq r
, let
h_{i}:\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to\textup{Bun}_{G}
record
\mathcal{F}_{i}
. Let
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\subset\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
be the preimage of
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
under
h_{0}
.

The
moduli stack
\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
of
G
-shtukas with
r
legs and modification type
\mu
is the fiber product

(5.2.2)

\textstyle{\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(h_{0},h_{r})}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\textup{id},\textup{Fr})}
\textstyle{\textup{Bun}_{G}\times\textup{Bun}_{G}}

We decompose
\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
according to which connected component of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
the first bundle
\mathcal{E}_{0}
lies in. This gives a decomposition

\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}=\coprod_{\omega\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})}{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}

so that
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
is the preimage of
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
. Let

(5.2.3)

\omega_{j}=\omega+\overline{\mu}_{1}+\cdots+\overline{\mu}_{j}\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G}),\quad j=1,2,\cdots,r.

Then for
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{r},\mathcal{F}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{r}\cong{}^{\tau}\mathcal{F}_{0})\in{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{j}\in\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega_{j}}
.

5.2.2.
Definition of Arithmetic volume

For
j=1,2,\ldots,r
, let
D_{\mu_{j}}=\dim(G/P_{\mu_{j}})=\langle{2\rho,\mu_{j}}\rangle
and choose a pair of classes

\eta_{j}\in R^{W_{\mu_{j}}}_{2(D_{\mu_{j}}+1)},\quad\eta^{\prime}_{j}\in R^{W_{\mu_{j}}}_{2D_{\mu_{j}}}.

We have a composite morphism

\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}\to\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu}\to\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{j}}.

Composing this further with the map
\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{j}}\xrightarrow{\textup{ev}_{j}}\mathbb{B}P_{\mu_{j}}
from (
5.1.4
) (corresponding to the canonical parabolic reduction of
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}|_{\Gamma(x_{j})}
), we obtain the map

\textup{ev}_{j}:\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu_{j}}\xrightarrow{\textup{ev}_{j}}\mathbb{B}P_{\mu_{j}}.

For
1\leq j\leq r
, let
p_{j}:\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}\to X
be the map recording the
j
th leg.

We let
\eta
be the
r
-tuple
(\eta_{1}+\xi\eta_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\eta_{r}+\xi\eta_{r}^{\prime})
. Recall the operators
{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta_{j}+\xi\eta^{\prime}_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
defined in (
5.1.9
). Write

{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu}:={}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta_{r}+\xi\eta^{\prime}_{r}}_{\mu_{r}}\circ\ldots\circ{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta_{1}+\xi\eta^{\prime}_{1}}_{\mu_{1}}\colon\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega_{r}})=\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})

for the composition of the
{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta_{j}+\xi\eta^{\prime}_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
; note that the target component
\omega
agrees with the source component
\omega
by the assumption (
5.2.1
).

Definition 5.2.3
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{r})
and
\eta=(\eta_{1}+\xi\eta_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\eta_{r}+\xi\eta_{r}^{\prime})
. The
arithmetic volume
of
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
with respect to
\eta
is the
graded
trace

(5.2.4)

\textup{vol}({}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}_{G}^{\mu},\eta):=\textup{Tr}({}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu}\circ\textup{Frob}\mid\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})).

Since
\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})
is infinite-dimensional, it is not clear that the RHS is well-defined; this will be justified in Proposition
5.5.3
.

Remark 5.2.4
.

We justify why the definition (
5.2.4
) deserves to be viewed as an “arithmetic volume”. The numerics are arranged so that
\prod_{j=1}^{r}(\textup{ev}_{j}^{*}\eta_{j}+p_{j}^{*}\xi\cdot\textup{ev}_{j}^{*}\eta^{\prime}_{j})
is a top-degree cohomology class of
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
. If
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
were proper, then

(5.2.5)

\int_{{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}(\textup{ev}_{j}^{*}\eta_{j}+p_{j}^{*}\xi\cdot\textup{ev}_{j}^{*}\eta^{\prime}_{j})

would be well-defined, as the evaluation of the fundamental class of
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
on the top-degree cohomology class, and we would take this to be
\textup{vol}(\textup{Sht}_{G}^{\mu},\eta)
. But
{}^{\omega}\textup{Sht}^{\mu}_{G}
is almost never proper, so (
5.2.5
) has no a priori meaning.

If
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
were proper, then a suitable version of the Grothendieck–Lefschetz trace formula (e.g.,
[
FYZ24
, Proposition 11.8]
) would identify (
5.2.5
) with
\textup{Tr}(\textup{Frob}\circ{}_{c}\Gamma^{\eta}_{\mu}\mid\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}))
. The latter expression has the potential at least to make sense when
\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
is not proper (there is a convergence question, because
\mathrm{H}_{c}^{*}(\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega})
is infinite-dimensional), and we will show that it indeed is always well-defined.

Our main theorem (Theorem
5.6.9
) computes the arithmetic volume in terms of differential operators applied to the
L
-function of the Gross motive of
G
(cf. §
4.1.8
). These differential operators are determined by local data, which we describe next.

5.3.
Local operators on characteristic classes

We define some “local” operators on
R^{W}=\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}G})
.

5.3.1.
The operator
\nabla^{\eta}_{\mu}

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Then we have the partial derivative
\partial_{\mu}
on
R=\textup{Sym}(\mathbb{X}^{*}(T)_{\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}}(-1))
which lowers the grading by
2
and twist by
1
. It carries the subring
R^{W}
to
R^{W_{\mu}}
, so we may view it as a derivation

\partial_{\mu}:R^{W}\to R^{W_{\mu}}[-2](-1).

Let
P_{\mu}\subset G
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
corresponding to
\mu
. Pushforward along the proper smooth map
\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}G
induces an
R^{W}
-linear map

(5.3.1)

\int_{G/P_{\mu}}:R^{W_{\mu}}=\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}})\to\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}G})[-2D_{\mu}](-D_{\mu})=R^{W}[-2D_{\mu}](-D_{\mu})

where
D_{\mu}:=\dim(G/P_{\mu})=\langle 2\rho,\mu\rangle
.

Let
\eta\in R^{W_{\mu}}
be homogeneous of degree
2(D_{\mu}+1)
. Consider the map

(5.3.3)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\eta}_{\mu}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle R^{W}\to R^{W}

\displaystyle f\mapsto\int_{G/P_{\mu}}\eta\cdot\partial_{\mu}f.

Then
\nabla^{\eta}_{\mu}
is a degree-preserving derivation.

5.3.2.
The operator
\overline{\nabla}^{\eta}_{\mu}

Since
\nabla^{\eta}_{\mu}
is a derivation that carries
R^{W}_{+}
to
R^{W}_{+}
, it also sends
R^{W}_{+}\cdot R^{W}_{+}
to itself, hence induces a graded linear endomorphism of
\mathbb{V}

\overline{\nabla}^{\eta}_{\mu}\in\textup{End}^{gr}(\mathbb{V}).

In other words,
\overline{\nabla}^{\eta}_{\mu}
restricts to a linear endomorphism of each
\mathbb{V}_{d}
. The eigenvalues of
\overline{\nabla}^{\eta}_{\mu}
are the crucial “structure constants” for the upcoming formulas for arithmetic volumes. We refer to them as
eigenweights
.

Example 5.3.3
.

In many examples of interest,
\mathbb{V}_{2d}
has dimension at most one, and then the eigenweights can be calculated in the following way. By the assumption, we can choose homogeneous free generators
f_{1},f_{2},\cdots,f_{n}
of
R^{W}
(which we know is a polynomial ring) of increasing degrees

2d_{1}<2d_{2}<\cdots<2d_{n}.

This means
\mathbb{V}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{V}_{2d_{i}}
and
\mathbb{V}_{2d_{i}}
is one-dimensional spanned by the image of
f_{i}
. Then, for degree reasons, we necessarily have for each
i=1,\cdots,n
,

\nabla_{\mu}^{\eta}(f_{i})=\epsilon_{i}(\eta,\mu)f_{i}+\mbox{(polynomial in $f_{1},\cdots,f_{i-1}$)}.

The numbers
\{\epsilon_{i}(\eta,\mu)\}_{i=1}^{n}
are the eigenweights of
\overline{\nabla}^{\eta}_{\mu}
.

5.4.
Example of calculating eigenweights

We will calculate the operator
\nabla^{\eta}_{\mu}
in the example
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
and
\mu=(1,0,\cdots,0)
, so that
D_{\mu}=n-1
. We identify
R=\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}]
(cf. Example
3.2.2
) with the obvious action of the Weyl group
W\cong S_{n}
by permutations on the variables, so that

R^{W}=\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}]

where the
e_{i}
are the elementary symmetric polynomials in the
x_{i}
.

Proposition 5.4.1
.

Let
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
and
\mu=(1,0,\ldots,0)
. Choose
\eta=x_{1}^{n}\in R^{W_{\mu}}
. Then we have

\nabla_{\mu}^{\eta}(e_{i})=(-1)^{n-1}e_{i}\text{ for all }i=1,2,\ldots,n.

In particular, we have

\epsilon_{i}(\eta,\mu)=(-1)^{n-1}
for each
i=1,\ldots,n
.

Example 5.4.2
.

For
i=1
, we want to calculate

\nabla_{\mu}^{\eta}(e_{1})=\int_{G/P_{\mu}}x_{1}^{n}.

An
R^{W}
-module basis for
R^{W_{\mu}}
is given by the powers
x_{1}^{0},\ldots,x_{1}^{n-1}
, and
\int_{G/P_{\mu}}
extracts the coefficients of
(-x_{1})^{n-1}
, by our convention that
-x_{1}
corresponds to
\mathcal{O}(1)
on
G/P_{\mu}
(cf. Example
2.3.1
). We have the characteristic relation

x_{1}^{n}-e_{1}x_{1}^{n-1}+\ldots+(-1)^{n}e_{n}=0.

Thus

\int_{G/P_{\mu}}x_{1}^{n}=\int_{G/P_{\mu}}(e_{1}x_{1}^{n-1}+\ldots)=(-1)^{n-1}e_{1}.

For
i>1
, the combinatorics become much more complicated to analyze directly in this way, so we will first develop some machinery.

5.4.3.
Combinatorial formula for the integration map

We will first give a general description of the integration map (
5.3.1
) in more concrete terms, which will be useful for explicit calculations.

We maintain the notation of §
5.3.1
, so
G
is an arbitrary split reductive over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
. Consider the
G
-equivariant top Chern class of the tangent bundle of
G/P_{\mu}

(5.4.1)

c_{D_{\mu}}(T_{G/P_{\mu}})=\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}:=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\Phi(G)\\
\langle{\alpha,\mu}\rangle<0\end{subarray}}\alpha\in R^{W_{\mu}}

where
\Phi(G)
is the set of roots of
G
.

Lemma 5.4.4
.

For any
f\in R^{W_{\mu}}
, we have

(5.4.2)

\int_{G/P_{\mu}}f=\sum_{w\in W/W_{\mu}}w(f/\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}).

Here the sum is over a set of representatives of
W/W_{\mu}
, which is well-defined since
f/\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}
is
W_{\mu}
-invariant. (The element
f/\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}
is understood in the fraction field of
R
, yet the sum above will lie in
R^{W}
.)

Proof.

Suppose the Lemma is proved whenever the parabolic subgroup is a Borel. Let
B
be the standard Borel subgroup contained in
P_{\mu}
. Let
B_{\mu}:=B\cap L_{\mu}
, a Borel subgroup of
L_{\mu}
. Let
\mathfrak{R}^{\mu}
be the analogous construction for
B_{\mu}<L_{\mu}
(i.e., the
L_{\mu}
-equivariant top Chern class of
L_{\mu}/B_{\mu})
, so that

\mathfrak{R}:=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\Phi(G)\\
\alpha<0\end{subarray}}\alpha=\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}^{\mu}\in R.

By assumption, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{G}^{*}(G/B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{L_{\mu}}^{*}(L_{\mu}/B_{\mu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{G}^{*}(G/P_{\mu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{L_{\mu}}^{*}(\textup{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{W_{\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{G}^{*}(\textup{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{w\in W_{\mu}}w((\cdot)/\mathfrak{R}^{\mu})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the composite right vertical arrow is
\sum_{w\in W}w((\cdot)/\mathfrak{R})
(which is the content of the lemma for
P_{\mu}=B
). The description of the map
R\to R^{W_{\mu}}
in the above diagram is the content of the lemma for
G=L_{\mu}
with its Borel
B_{\mu}
. This implies that the second right vertical arrow must be
\sum_{w\in W/W_{\mu}}w((\cdot)/\mathfrak{R}_{\mu})
.

So it suffices to verify the Lemma in the case where
P_{\mu}=B
is a Borel subgroup. By computing on simple reflections, which reduces to an
\textup{SL}_{2}
calculation, one checks that
\int_{G/B}
is sign-equivariant under the
W
-action:

\int_{G/B}wf=(-1)^{\ell(w)}\int_{G/B}f,\quad\forall f\in R.

Let
R^{\textup{sgn}}
be the sign-isotypic component of
R
under the
W
-action. Let

\displaystyle\textup{Av}_{\textup{sgn}}:R

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle R^{\textup{sgn}}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|W|}\sum_{w\in W}(-1)^{\ell(w)}wf

be the projector to
R^{\textup{sgn}}
. Since
\int_{G/B}
is sign-equivariant, it has a factorization as a composition

\int_{G/B}:R\xrightarrow{\textup{Av}_{\textup{sgn}}}R^{\textup{sgn}}\xrightarrow{\iota}R^{W}

for a unique
R^{W}
-linear map
\iota
. It is well-known that
R^{\textup{sgn}}
is a free
R^{W}
-module with basis
\mathfrak{R}
; hence, to determine
\iota
, it suffices to compute
\iota(\mathfrak{R})
.

By the definition of
\int_{G/B}
as a pushforward, we have

\int_{G/B}\mathfrak{R}=\int_{G/B}c_{\dim G/B}(T_{G/B})=\chi(G/B)=|W|.

This implies
\iota(\mathfrak{R})=|W|
, which in turn implies that

\int_{G/B}f=\iota(\textup{Av}_{\textup{sgn}}(f))=|W|\textup{Av}_{\textup{sgn}}(f)/\mathfrak{R}=\sum_{w\in W}w(f/\mathfrak{R}),

proving (
5.4.2
).
∎

5.4.5.
Proof of Proposition
5.4.1

Now we return to the setup of Proposition
5.4.1
, so
G=\textup{GL}_{n}
and
\mu=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{X}_{*}(T)
.

We have arranged that
\partial_{\mu}=\partial_{x_{1}}
. We choose
\eta=x_{1}^{n}
. In this case,
\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}G
is a
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
-bundle with
x_{1}
being the first Chern class of the tautological bundle
\mathcal{O}(-1)
in our conventions (cf. Example
2.3.1
).

Each
e_{i}
spans the (1-dimensional) eigenspaces
\mathbb{V}_{2d_{i}}
, and we want to calculate

\nabla_{\mu}^{\eta}(e_{i})=\int_{G/P_{\mu}}x_{1}^{n}\partial_{x_{1}}e_{i}.

Let
\widehat{e}_{i}\in R
be the
i
th elementary symmetric polynomial in
x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}
(omitting
x_{1}
). Note that
\partial_{x_{1}}(e_{i})=\widehat{e}_{i-1}
, so that we are interested in calculating
\int_{G/P_{\mu}}x_{1}^{n}\cdot\widehat{e}_{i-1}
.

Let
t
be a formal variable. Then we have

(5.4.3)

\prod_{j=2}^{n}(t-x_{j})=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{i}t^{n-1-i}\widehat{e}_{i}.

Identifying
W=S_{n}
with the permutations of the
\{x_{i}\}
, the subgroup
W_{\mu}=S_{n-1}
is the stabilizer of
x_{1}
. We claim that

(5.4.4)

\sum_{w\in S_{n}/S_{n-1}}w\left(x_{1}^{n}\frac{\prod_{j=2}^{n}(t-x_{j})}{\prod_{j=2}^{n}(x_{1}-x_{j})}\right)=t^{n}-\prod_{i=1}^{n}(t-x_{i}).

Indeed, the left side is the Lagrange interpolation formula for the unique degree
n-1
polynomial
P(t)
such that
P(x_{i})=x_{i}^{n}
for each
i=1,\ldots,n
, and the right side visibly has this same property, so they must agree.

Comparing coefficients of
t^{n-i}
in (
5.4.4
), and using (
5.4.3
), yields

(5.4.5)

\sum_{w\in S_{n}/S_{n-1}}w\left(\frac{x_{1}^{n}\cdot\widehat{e}_{i-1}}{\prod_{j=2}^{n}(x_{1}-x_{j})}\right)=e_{i}.

Note that the denominator
\prod_{j=2}^{n}(x_{1}-x_{j})
is
(-1)^{n-1}\mathfrak{R}_{\mu}
from (
5.4.1
). Invoking Lemma
5.4.4
, we find that

(5.4.6)

\nabla_{\mu}^{\eta}(e_{i})=\int_{G/P_{\mu}}x_{1}^{n}\widehat{e}_{i-1}{=}(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{w\in S_{n}/S_{n-1}}w\left(\frac{x_{1}^{n}\cdot\widehat{e}_{i-1}}{\prod_{j=2}^{n}(x_{1}-x_{j})}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:eigenweight-minimal-example}}}{{=}}(-1)^{n-1}e_{i},

as desired. ∎

5.5.
Convergence of trace

Since the definition of arithmetic volume involves taking trace on an infinite-dimensional vector space, some analysis is required to see that the trace actually converges to a well-defined number; we undertake this analysis now in a more general context.

5.5.1.
More general arithmetic volumes

Fix an admissible sequence
\mu=(\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{r})
of dominant minuscule coweights of
T
. Recall
N=\sum_{j=1}^{r}(D_{\mu_{j}}+1)
. For any
\theta\in\textup{H}^{2N}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
, we can define the operator
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
on
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
similarly as in §
5.1.2
as the composition

\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}:=h_{0*}(h_{r}^{*}(-)\cup\theta):\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\to\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}).

Similarly we have the version with compact support

{}_{c}\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}:=h_{r*}(h_{0}^{*}(-)\cup\theta):\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\to\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}).

Lemma 5.5.2
.

For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\textup{Tr}({}_{c}\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}\circ\textup{Frob}\mid\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}))=q^{\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}}\textup{Tr}(\textup{Frob}^{-1}\circ\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}\mid\textup{H}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}-i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})).

Proof.

By the analog of (
5.1.12
), under the Poincaré duality pairing (
5.1.11
), the endomorphism
{}_{c}\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
of
\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
is adjoint to the endomorphism
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
of
\textup{H}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}-i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. Also, the automorphism
Frob
on
\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
is adjoint to the automorphism
q^{\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}}\textup{Frob}^{-1}
on
\textup{H}^{2\dim\textup{Bun}_{G}-i}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
. The conclusion follows.
∎

Proposition 5.5.3
.

Fix an embedding of fields
\iota:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}
. For any
\theta\in\textup{H}^{2N}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
, the two series of complex numbers

(5.5.1)

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i}\iota\left(\textup{Tr}(\textup{Frob}^{-1}\circ\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}|\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}))\right)\mbox{ and }\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i}\iota\left(\textup{Tr}({}_{c}\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}\circ\textup{Frob}|\textup{H}_{c}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}))\right)

are absolutely convergent. We denote their sums by

\textup{Tr}_{\iota}(\textup{Frob}^{-1}\circ\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}|\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}))\mbox{ and }\textup{Tr}_{\iota}({}_{c}\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}\circ\textup{Frob}|\textup{H}_{c}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}))

respectively.

By Lemma
5.5.2
, it suffices to prove the absolute convergence of the first series in (
5.5.1
), whose proof occupies the rest of the subsection.

5.5.4.
The Ran grading

For
m\geq 0
define
\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\subset\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
to be the subspace spanned by elements of the form

f_{1}^{z_{1}}f_{2}^{z_{2}}\cdots f_{s}^{z_{s}}\quad\mbox{such that $\sum_{i}|z_{i}|=m$ and $\deg(f_{i})>|z_{i}|$ for each $i=1,\cdots,s$.}

Here the
f_{i}\in R^{W}_{+}
are homogeneous. We note that
\deg(f_{i})>|z_{i}|
automatically holds unless
\deg(f_{i})=2=|z_{i}|
, which is not allowed in the description of the Atiyah–Bott formula §
4.2.9
(for in this case
f_{i}^{z_{i}}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
).

From the definition, it is clear that
\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
are multiplicative under cup product in that

\mathfrak{R}_{m}\mathfrak{R}_{l}\subset\mathfrak{R}_{m+l}.

Lemma 5.5.5
.

We have a decomposition

(5.5.2)

\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})\cong\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}).

We call the resulting
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
-grading on
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}})
the Ran grading
8
8

8

The nomenclature comes from the fact that
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
is the factorization homology of
\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}G})
, a key observation of
[
GL14
]
.
.

Proof.

Abbreviate
\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
simply by
\mathfrak{R}_{m}
. Let
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}
be a set of free homogeneous generators of
R^{W}
. Let
z_{0}=1\in\textup{H}_{0}({X})
,
z_{1},\cdots,z_{2g}\in\textup{H}_{1}({X})
, and
z_{2g+1}=[X]\in\textup{H}_{2}({X})
be a homogeneous basis of
\textup{H}_{*}({X})
. Then by Atiyah-Bott formula (see §
4.2.9
),
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
has a
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
-basis consisting of

(5.5.3)

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=0}^{2g+1}(f_{i}^{z_{j}})^{e_{ij}}\quad\text{ where }\quad e_{ij}\begin{cases}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}&\mbox{if $j=0$ or $j=2g+1$},\\
\in\{0,1\}&\mbox{if $j=1,\cdots,2g$}.\end{cases}

Let
\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
be the subspace spanned by those monomials of the form (
5.5.3
) where

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{2g}e_{ij}+2e_{i,2g+1}\Big)=m.

Clearly
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
is the direct sum of
\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
. It remains to show that
\mathfrak{R}_{m}=\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
. The inclusion
\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}\subset\mathfrak{R}_{m}
is clear. Conversely, to show that
\mathfrak{R}_{m}\subset\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
, we observe that
\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
are also multiplicative, so it suffices to show that

(5.5.4)

f^{z}\in\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{|z|}

for any
f\in R^{W}
and homogeneous
z\in\textup{H}_{*}({X})
.

Any
f\in R^{W}
can be written as
f=P(f_{1},\cdots,f_{n})
for a unique polynomial
P
in
n
variables. We prove (
5.5.4
) by induction on the degree of
P
. The case
\deg(P)\leq 1
is clear. Suppose (
5.5.4
) holds for all
f
whose degree in
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}
is
<d
. Let
f=P(f_{1},\cdots,f_{n})
for
\deg(P)=d
. By linearity, it suffices to treat the case where
P
is a monomial of degree
d>1
. In this case we write
P=P_{1}P_{2}
where
P_{i}
are monomials of degree
<d
. Let
g_{1}=P_{1}(f_{1},\cdots,f_{n})
and
g_{2}=P_{2}(f_{1},\cdots,f_{n})
. Then by (
4.2.12
) we have

(5.5.5)

f^{z}=(g_{1}g_{2})^{z}=\sum_{j}(g_{1})^{z^{\prime}_{j}}(g_{2})^{z^{\prime\prime}_{j}}

where
\Delta_{*}(z)=\sum_{j}z^{\prime}_{j}\otimes z^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in\textup{H}_{|z|}({X\times X})
. By induction hypothesis
(g_{1})^{z^{\prime}_{j}}\in\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{|z^{\prime}_{j}|}
and
(g_{2})^{z^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\in\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{|z^{\prime\prime}_{j}|}
. Therefore (
5.5.5
) implies
f^{z}\in\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{|z|}
. This shows
\mathfrak{R}_{m}=\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}_{m}
, completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

For each
m
, define the
Ran filtration

F_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
on
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
by

(5.5.6)

F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})\cong\bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\leq m}\mathfrak{R}_{m^{\prime}}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}).

5.5.6.
Monomial basis for
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})

As in the proof of Lemma
5.5.5
, we now fix a choice of homogeneous free generators
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}
for
R^{W}
. Also choose a homogeneous basis
z_{0}=1,z_{1},\cdots,z_{2g},z_{2g+1}=[X]
for
\textup{H}_{*}({X})
that are also eigenvectors for the Frobenius action on
\textup{H}_{*}({X})
.
9
9

9

We are using here the fact that
Frob
acts semisimply on
\textup{H}_{1}({X})
; however, without using this fact, the argument can still be made with slight modification towards the end.
Let
\mathfrak{B}
be the set of elements of the form (
5.5.3
); they form a basis for
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
that we call
the monomial basis
.
We use the same notation
\mathfrak{B}
for varying components
\omega\in\pi_{0}(\textup{Bun}_{G})
.

For
\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}
in the form (
5.5.3
), we call

(5.5.7)

d:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq n,\ 0\leq j\leq 2g+1}e_{ij}

the
naive degree
of
\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}
.

5.5.7.
Hecke action on tautological classes

From here until the end of Lemma
5.5.15
, we will fix a dominant minuscule coweight
\mu
of
G
, and consider the one-step Hecke stack
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
.

First we need to prove a technical result about the behavior of tautological classes under Hecke correspondences. We follow the notation of §
5.1.1
.

Proposition 5.5.8
.

For
f\in R^{W}
and
z\in\textup{H}_{|z|}({X})
, we have

h_{1}^{*}(f^{z})-h_{0}^{*}(f^{z})=\textup{PD}(z)\partial_{\mu}(f)+\begin{cases}(1-g)\langle{z,\xi}\rangle\xi\partial_{\mu}^{2}(f)&|z|=2,\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

We apply Theorem
3.2.1
to
S:=X\times{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
, the two
G
-bundles
\mathcal{F}_{0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{1}
with the modification (
5.1.3
) along the divisor
D=\Gamma(p_{X})
. Note that
D\hookrightarrow S
is the pullback of the diagonal
\Delta_{X}\hookrightarrow X\times X
under
(q_{X},p_{X}\circ p_{\mathrm{Hk}}):S\to X\times X
. Therefore the normal bundle of
D
is the pullback
p_{X}^{*}T_{X}
. In particular, its first Chern class is
\nu_{D}=(2-2g)\xi
, and
\nu_{D}^{2}=0
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the
L_{\mu}
-torsor on
\Gamma(p_{X})
obtained from the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{0}|_{\Gamma(p_{X})}
(cf. §
3.1.3
). Since
\nu_{D}^{2}=0
as noted above, Theorem
3.2.1
says in this case that

(5.5.8)

f(\mathcal{F}_{1})-f(\mathcal{F}_{0})=i_{D!}\left((\partial_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H})+\frac{1}{2}(\partial^{2}_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H})\cdot\nu_{D}\right).

By the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{k}{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Id\times p_{X}}

we have

i_{D!}(\partial_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H})=i_{D!}i_{D}^{*}(p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}(\partial_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H}))=[D]\cdot p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}((\partial_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H}))=[\Delta_{X}]\cdot p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}((\partial_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{H})).

By our convention (
5.1.5
), we are viewing
R^{W}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathrm{Hk}_{\mu}^{\omega})
embedded via the classifying map for
\mathcal{H}
, so we can simply write
\partial_{\mu}f(\mathcal{H})
as
\partial_{\mu}f\in\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
, which we will do below. Similarly, using
\nu_{D}=(2-2g)\xi
we have

\displaystyle i_{D!}\Big(\frac{1}{2}(\partial^{2}_{\mu}f)\nu_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle=i_{D!}i_{D}^{*}(p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}((1-g)\xi\partial^{2}_{\mu}f))=[D]\cdot p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}((1-g)\xi\partial^{2}_{\mu}f)

\displaystyle=(1-g)[\Delta_{X}]\cdot(1\otimes\xi)p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}(\partial^{2}_{\mu}f)=(1-g)(\xi\otimes\xi)p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}(\partial^{2}_{\mu}f)

where in the last step we used that
[\Delta_{X}]\cdot(1\otimes\xi)=(\xi\otimes\xi)\in\textup{H}^{4}({X\times X})(2)
. Plugging these into (
5.5.8
) we get

f(\mathcal{F}_{1})-f(\mathcal{F}_{0})=[\Delta_{X}]\cdot p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}(\partial_{\mu}f)+(1-g)(\xi\otimes\xi)p_{\mathrm{Hk}}^{*}(\partial^{2}_{\mu}f).

Now we expand both sides using Künneth formula for
S=X\times{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
, and use
[\Delta_{X}]=\xi\otimes 1-\beta+1\otimes\xi
; then contracting with
z\in\mathrm{H}_{*}(X)
gives the desired formula.
∎

5.5.9.
Ran filtration for Hecke stack

We also define an increasing filtration on
\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
as follows. Let

\displaystyle F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=0,\quad m<-2,

\displaystyle F_{-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=p_{X}^{*}\textup{H}^{2}({X})\cdot R^{W_{\mu}},

\displaystyle F_{-1}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=p_{X}^{*}\textup{H}^{\geq 1}({X})\cdot R^{W_{\mu}},

\displaystyle F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=p_{X}^{*}\textup{H}^{*}({X})\cdot R^{W_{\mu}},\quad\mbox{ which is the image of \eqref{taut class Hk}}

\displaystyle F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})\cdot h_{0}^{*}F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}),\quad m\geq 1.

Both filtrations
F_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
and
F_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
are also multiplicative, i.e.,

F_{i}\cdot F_{j}\subset F_{i+j}.

In particular,
F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
(resp.
F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
) is a subring of
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
(resp.
\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
).

Lemma 5.5.10
.

The map
h_{1}^{*}
sends
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})
to
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Consider
f^{z}\in F_{|z|}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})
. Then by Proposition
5.5.8
we have

(5.5.9)

h_{1}^{*}(f^{z})-h_{0}^{*}(f^{z})\in p_{X}^{*}\textup{H}^{\geq 2-|z|}({X})\cdot R^{W_{\mu}}=F_{|z|-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}).

Since
h_{0}^{*}(f^{z})\in F_{|z|}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
by definition, (
5.5.9
) implies that
h_{1}^{*}(f^{z})\in F_{|z|}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
.

By construction, both filtrations on
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})
and on
\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
are multiplicative. Since
h_{1}^{*}
preserves the filtrations on a set of ring generators, it preserves the filtrations on all elements of
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})
.
∎

Theorem
4.2.8
gives a canonical identification of the cohomology rings of different components of
\textup{Bun}_{G}
. Using this identification, it makes sense to define a map

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mu}:\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle h_{1}^{*}\theta-h_{0}^{*}\theta.

Lemma 5.5.11
.

The map
\Delta_{\mu}
sends
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
to
F_{m-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. The induced map on the associated graded

\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}\Delta_{\mu}:\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\to\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

is a derivation with respect to the ring homomorphism

\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}(h_{0}^{*})=\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}(h_{1}^{*}):\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\cong\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega^{\prime}}_{G}})\to\textup{Gr}^{F}_{\bullet}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}).

It is characterized by the property that

(\textup{Gr}^{F}_{|z|}\Delta_{\mu})(f^{z})=\textup{PD}(z)\partial_{\mu}(f)\in\textup{Gr}^{F}_{|z|-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}).

Proof.

By definition, a general element in
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
is a linear combination of
f=f_{1}^{z_{1}}f_{2}^{z_{2}}\cdots f_{s}^{z_{s}}
where
|z_{1}|+\cdots+|z_{s}|\leq m
. We may write

(5.5.10)

h_{1}^{*}f-h_{0}^{*}f=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}<i}h_{1}^{*}f_{i^{\prime}}^{z_{i^{\prime}}}\right)(h_{1}^{*}f_{i}^{z_{i}}-h_{0}^{*}f_{i}^{z_{i}})\left(\prod_{i^{\prime\prime}>i}h_{0}^{*}f_{i^{\prime\prime}}^{z_{i^{\prime\prime}}}\right).

By Lemma
5.5.10
and (
5.5.9
), each summand above lies in
F_{\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}|z_{i^{\prime}}|+|z_{i}|-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})=F_{m-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
. Passing to associated graded, we learn that
h_{0}^{*}f
has the same image as
h_{1}^{*}f
in
\textup{Gr}^{F}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
, hence (
5.5.10
) tells that
\Delta_{\mu}
is a derivation after passing to associated graded. The calculation of
(\textup{Gr}^{F}_{|z|}\Delta_{\mu})(f^{z})
follows from Proposition
5.5.8
.
∎

Lemma 5.5.12
.

The map
h_{0*}
carries
F_{m-2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})\rightarrow F_{m}\textup{H}^{*-2D_{\mu}-2}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})(-D_{\mu}-1)
for all
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

The canonical parabolic reduction fits into a commutative diagram

(5.5.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{B}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}\times h_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{ev}^{\omega}}

whose top square is moreover Cartesian, and whose vertical maps are fiber bundles for
G/P_{\mu}
. Therefore, using the notation
\int_{G/P_{\mu}}:R^{W_{\mu}}\to R^{W}
introduced in §
5.3.1
, we can rewrite
h_{0*}
on the image of
\textup{H}^{*}({X})\otimes\textup{H}^{*}({\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}})=\textup{H}^{*}({X})\otimes R^{W_{\mu}}\subset\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
as the composition

(5.5.12)

h_{0*}:\textup{H}^{*}({X})\otimes R^{W_{\mu}}\xrightarrow{\textup{id}\otimes\int_{G/P_{\mu}}}\textup{H}^{*}({X})\otimes R^{W}\xrightarrow{\textup{id}\otimes\textup{ev}^{\omega,*}}\textup{H}^{*}({X\times\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})\xrightarrow{\int_{X}}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}}).

For
n\leq 0
, we have

h_{0*}F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})=h_{0*}(\textup{H}^{\geq-m}(X)\otimes R^{W_{\mu}})\subset\int_{X}(\textup{H}^{\geq-m}(X)\cdot R^{W}).

Here
R^{W}
is embedded into
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
via
\textup{ev}^{\omega,*}
. For
\zeta\in\textup{H}^{|\zeta|}({X})
and
f\in R^{W}
,
\int_{X}(\zeta f)
is a linear combination of
f^{z}
where
|z|+|\zeta|=2
. This implies

\int_{X}(\textup{H}^{\geq-m}(X)\cdot R^{W})\subset F_{m+2}.

Now consider the case
m>0
. We abbreviate
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}^{\omega}_{G}})
simply as
F_{m}
.
Using that
h_{0*}
is linear over
h_{0}^{*}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
, and
h_{0*}F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})\subset F_{2}
by the preceding paragraph, we conclude that

\displaystyle h_{0*}F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})

\displaystyle=h_{0*}(F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})\cdot h_{0}^{*}F_{m})

\displaystyle=h_{0*}F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})\cdot F_{m}\subset F_{2}\cdot F_{m}\subset F_{m+2}.

∎

Corollary 5.5.13
.

For any
\beta\in\textup{H}^{*}({X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}})
,
\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}
sends
F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega^{\prime}}})
to
F_{\leq m+2}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha\in F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega^{\prime}}})
. By Lemma
5.5.10
,
h_{1}^{*}\alpha\in F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu}})
. By definition,
\beta\in F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu}})
, hence
h_{1}^{*}(\alpha)\beta\in F_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu}})
. Finally, by Lemma
5.5.12
,
\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)=h_{0*}(h_{1}^{*}(\alpha)\beta)\in F_{\leq m+2}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}_{G}^{\mu}})
.
∎

5.5.14.
Truncation of
\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}

Let
\beta\in\textup{H}^{*}({X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu}})
be homogeneous. For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
let
\mathfrak{R}_{i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}:\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega^{\prime}}})\to\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
be the Ran degree
i
part of
\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}
: for
\alpha\in\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega^{\prime}}})
,
\mathfrak{R}_{i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)
is the Ran degree
m+i
piece of
\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)
. Define
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}:\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega^{\prime}}})\to\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
to be the sum

\mathfrak{R}_{\geq i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}=\sum_{i^{\prime}\geq i}\mathfrak{R}_{i^{\prime}}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}.

The main estimate to prove the convergence of trace is the following.

Lemma 5.5.15
.

Fix
i\geq 0
. For a monomial basis element
\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}
, write
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}
as a linear combination of the monomial basis for
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
:

\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}}c^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\alpha^{\prime}

for
c^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{C}
. Then there is
C_{\beta,\mu,i}>0
depending only on
\beta,\mu,i
(and not on
\alpha
) such that

\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}}|c^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\alpha}|_{\iota}\leq C_{\beta,\mu,i}(d+1)^{i/2+1},\mbox{ for all $\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}$ with naive degree $d$}.

Here
|\cdot|_{\iota}
denotes the absolute value under the embedding
\iota:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}\to\mathbf{C}
.

Proof.

Write
\alpha=\prod_{s=1}^{d}f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}}
, where
d
is the naive degree. Let
m=\sum_{s=1}^{d}|z_{j_{s}}|
be the Ran degree of
\alpha
. Let
\Delta_{\mu}(f^{z})=h_{1}^{*}(f^{z})-h_{0}^{*}(f^{z})
. Expanding
h_{1}^{*}\alpha=\prod_{s}h_{1}^{*}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})=\prod_{s}(h_{0}^{*}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})+\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}}))
, we get

(5.5.13)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{0*}(h_{1}^{*}\alpha\cdot\beta)=\sum_{J\subset\{1,2,\cdots,d\}}\pm h_{0*}\left(h_{0}^{*}(\prod_{s\notin J}f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\cdot\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\beta\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{J\subset\{1,2,\cdots,d\}}\pm\left(\prod_{s\notin J}f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}}\right)h_{0*}(\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\beta).

By Lemma
5.5.11
,
\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\in F_{\sum_{s\in J}(|z_{j_{s}}|-2)}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
. Since
\beta\in F_{0}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
, by Lemma
5.5.12
, we have

h_{0*}(\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\beta)\in F_{\leq 2+\sum_{s\in J}(|z_{j_{s}}|-2)}\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}}).

Therefore, the
J
-summand of (
5.5.13
) lies in
F_{\leq m-2|J|+2}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. Therefore, in computing
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)
, we only need to sum over those
J\subset\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
with
m-2|J|+2\geq m-i
, i.e.,
|J|\leq i/2+1
. The number of such
J
is
\leq(d+1)^{i/2+1}
. For fixed
J
, writing
h_{0*}(\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})\beta)
as a sum of elements in
\mathfrak{B}
, the sum of absolute values of coefficients have an upper bound
C_{\beta,\mu,i}
, since there are only finitely many possibilities for
\prod_{s\in J}\Delta_{\mu}(f_{i_{s}}^{z_{j_{s}}})
when
|J|
is bounded by
i
. Therefore for each
|J|\leq i/2+1
, the corresponding summand in (
5.5.13
) will contribute at most
C_{\beta,\mu,i}
to the total sum of absolute values of coefficients of
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\mu}(\alpha)
, giving the desired upper bound.
∎

5.5.16.
Proof of Proposition
5.5.3
–first reductions

The map
(p_{1},\cdots,p_{r},h_{0}):{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to X^{r}\times\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}
is an iterated fibration with fibers
G/P_{\mu_{j}}
. Therefore, as a module over
h_{0}^{*}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
,
\textup{H}^{*}({{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}})
is generated by elements of the form
\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{r}
, where
\beta_{j}\in\textup{H}^{*}({X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu_{j}}})
is pulled back to
{}^{\omega}\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}
via
(p_{j},\textup{ev}_{j}):\mathrm{Hk}^{\mu}_{G}\to X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu_{j}}
.
It is therefore sufficient to treat the case where
\theta=(h_{0}^{*}\alpha)\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{r}
, where
\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}
is a monomial basis element for
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
and
\beta_{i}\in\textup{H}^{*}({X\times\mathbb{B}P_{\mu_{i}}})
. In this case
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
is the composition

\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}=(\cup\alpha)\circ\Gamma^{\beta_{1}}_{\mu_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\Gamma^{\beta_{r}}_{\mu_{r}}.

Here
\cup\alpha
is the endomorphism of
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
given by cup product with
\alpha
.

Below we base change all
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
-vector spaces to
\mathbf{C}
-vector spaces using
\iota
. In particular,
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
can be represented as a matrix with entries in
\mathbf{C}
under the monomial basis of
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. We can therefore talk about the diagonal matrix coefficients of
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
.

Lemma 5.5.17
.

Let
t
be the Ran degree of
\alpha
, and let
u=r(t/2+r)
. There exists
C_{\theta,\mu}>0
(depending on
\theta
and
\mu
) such that, when writing
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
as a matrix using the monomial basis
\mathfrak{B}
, its diagonal entry at any
\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}
with naive degree
d\geq 0
has absolute value bounded above by
C_{\theta,\mu}(d+1)^{u}
.

Proof.

Let
i=t+2(r-1)
. Write each
\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
as
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}+\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
(where
\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
is the part of
\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
that decreases the Ran degree by more than
i
). We expand

\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}=(\cup\alpha)\circ(\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{1}}_{\mu_{1}}+\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{1}}_{\mu_{1}})\circ\cdots\circ(\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{r}}_{\mu_{r}}+\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{r}}_{\mu_{r}})

into a sum of compositions of
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
with
\cup\alpha
. If
\mathfrak{R}_{<-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j}}_{\mu_{j}}
appears at the
j
th place, then the corresponding term will send
\mathfrak{R}_{m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
to
\mathfrak{R}_{<m-i+2(r-1)+t}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})=\mathfrak{R}_{<m}\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
, because each of the other terms
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{j^{\prime}}}_{\mu_{j^{\prime}}}
(there are
\leq r-1
of them) can at most increase the Ran grading by
2
, and
\cup\alpha
increases the Ran grading by
m
. Therefore, under the monomial basis, the diagonal entries of
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
are the same as the diagonal entries of

(5.5.14)

(\cup\alpha)\circ\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{1}}_{\mu_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{r}}_{\mu_{r}}.

Now if
\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}
has naive degree
d
, by iterative use of Lemma
5.5.15
, the sum of absolute values of coefficients of
\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{1}}_{\mu_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{R}_{\geq-i}\Gamma^{\beta_{r}}_{\mu_{r}}(\alpha^{\prime})
in terms of
\mathfrak{B}
is bounded above by

(5.5.15)

\prod_{s=1}^{r}\left(C_{\beta_{s},\mu_{s},i}(d+1+2(s-1))^{i/2+1}\right).

Since
\alpha\in\mathfrak{B}
, all matrix entries of
\cup\alpha
in terms of the basis
\mathfrak{B}
are either
0
or
1
, we conclude that all diagonal entries of (
5.5.14
) are bounded by (
5.5.15
). Therefore the same is true for the diagonal entries of
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
. For a suitable constant
C_{\theta,\mu}
depending only on
\theta
and
\mu
, one can bound (
5.5.15
) from above by
C_{\theta,\mu}(d+1)^{r(i/2+1)}=C(d+1)^{u}
.
∎

5.5.18.
Finish of the proof of Proposition
5.5.3

Now decompose
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
according to the naive degrees of monomial basis elements:

\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\mathfrak{N}_{d},

where
\mathfrak{N}_{d}
is the span of
\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}
with naive degree
d
. Note that this grading splits the augmentation filtration on
\textup{H}^{*}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
introduced in §
5.6.14
. All the generators
f_{i}^{z_{j}}\in\mathfrak{B}
have cohomological degrees between
2
and
2d_{n}
, where
d_{n}
is the largest degree of the generators
\{f_{j}\}
of
R^{W}
. Hence a monomial of naive degree
d
has degree in
[2d,2d_{n}d]
. Therefore

\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})\subset\bigoplus_{i/(2d_{n})\leq d\leq i/2}\mathfrak{N}_{d}.

In particular, by Lemma
5.5.17
, the diagonal entries of
\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}
on
\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
are bounded by
C(i/2+1)^{u}
. The Frobenius action is diagonal with respect to the monomial basis, and has eigenvalues with absolute value
q^{i/2}
on
\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
. Therefore

(5.5.16)

|\textup{Tr}(\textup{Frob}^{-1}\circ\Gamma^{\theta}_{\mu}|\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}))|\leq C_{\theta,\mu}q^{-i/2}(i/2+1)^{u}\dim\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}}).

Now
(i/2+1)^{u}\dim\textup{H}^{i}({\textup{Bun}_{G}^{\omega}})
has polynomial growth in
i
while
q^{-i/2}
decays exponentially, so the summation of (
5.5.16
) over
i
converges. This finishes the proof of Proposition
5.5.3
.
∎

5.6.
Calculation of the arithmetic volume

We will compute the arithmetic volume defined in Definition
5.2.3
